## Interpretation and scoring

The thematic score is 0.40 × IDF-weighted shared-topic Jaccard + 0.20 × mean of the strongest three nonidentical co-occurrence bridges + 0.30 × mean of the strongest three independent abstract TF-IDF cosine similarities + 0.10 × strongest two-hop concept path. Missing components contribute zero; weights are not renormalised. TF-IDF uses unigrams and bigrams with smoothed IDF and L2 normalisation. Joint papers are excluded from abstract comparisons. These choices are transparent exploratory heuristics, not calibrated probabilities or mathematical proofs. Vocabulary entries normalise terminology; no mathematical relationships are seeded. Co-occurrence alone only creates candidate RELATED_TO edges. No GENERALISES, SPECIAL_CASE_OF or USES_METHOD edges are inferred without explicit reviewed evidence. Coauthorship and shared collaborators are reported separately from the thematic score. Shared broad concepts may inflate rankings; small samples, missing abstracts and provider author-cluster errors limit recall and precision.

## Review and evaluation

Use the application's Review tab or `python -m kgmap.cli export-reviews`, edit the JSON, then `import-reviews`. Review IDs persist across refreshes. Rejected extraction and concept-association edges are removed before visible scores are recomputed. Accepting a candidate records a mathematician's opinion; it does not turn similarity into a proven theorem. Corrected decisions require a note and preserve the original evidence. A scientific evaluation still needs a mathematician-labelled set: check author-cluster precision, topic extraction precision/recall, and connection usefulness, then compare against a shared-keywords-only baseline. No accuracy claim is made from software tests.
